### Page 3

통신서비스 Overweight

하나증권•3

2) 10월 투자 매력도 ‘높음’ 유지, 신규 주파수 할당/시중 금리 추이에 주목 
10월 통신주 투자 매력도 높음
으로 유지, 2027년 ARPU 성장 
기대감 생겨날 것임을 감안 시 
금리 하락 반전하면 투자 매력도 
높아질 전망

2026년 10월 통신서비스 업종 투자 매력도를 ‘높음’으로 유지한다. 물론 최근 정책 금리 
인상 및 시중 금리 상승으로 인해 통신주 배당 매력이 떨어지는 상황이긴 하다. 하지만 
연말부터는 다시 금리가 하락 반전할 가능성이 높고 2027년 국내에서 신규 주파수 경매 
및 새로운 5G 요금제 도입이 이루어질 경우 ARPU 상승 기대감이 높아질 수 있어 투자 
매력도가 높아질 것이란 판단이다. 
한국과 더불어 미국도 기준 금리를 인상하면서 투자가들에게 큰 충격을 주었다. 통신서
비스의 경우에도 영향이 적지 않은데 금융 업종과 달라 금리 인상에 따른 마진 변화가 
없는 반면 배당 투자 매력도가 떨어질 수 있어서 그렇다. 더불어 장기적으로 회사채 발
행 금리와 배당수익률간 차이가 없어질 경우 회사 입장에서 자사주 매입을 통한 배당 가
능 주식수 감소를 추진할 요인이 낮아지는 것도 문제점으로 지적된다. 그런데 현 시점에
서는 현재의 고금리 기조가 장기간 유지될 수 있을 지를 고민해 볼 필요가 있겠다. 미국
과 한국의 경기 상황을 감안할 때 그렇다. 2026년 말을 고점으로 시중 금리는 다시 하락
세로 반전할 가능성이 높고 2027년 서비스 매출액 성장 기대감이 높아질 수 있음을 감안
하면 4%대에 달하는 통신서비스 업종 기대배당수익률이 다시 매력적으로 변화될 수 있
을 것이란 판단이다. 
더불어 2027년도엔 국내에도 신규 주파수 경매와 더불어 5G 요금제 개편에 따른 ARPU 
상승 기대감이 생겨날 수 있다는 점에 주목할 필요가 있겠다. 미국이 이미 2026년 6월에 
이어 2027년 4월 주파수 경매를 확정함에 따라 총 700MHz에 달하는 5G 용도 주파수를 
확보한 상황이다. 그런데 한국은 2026년 12월 LTE 주파수를 5G로 일부 전환한다고 해도 
미국 대비 월등히 적은 5G 주파수 확보에 그칠 전망이다. 따라서 2027년 3.7GHz 대역 
300MHz 주파수를 5G 용도로 신규 공급할 가능성이 높고 망 투자 비용 부담을 명분으로 
5G 요금제 개편에 나설 공산이 커 보인다. 최근 전세계 주요 국가에선 서비스 품질에 따
른 통신 요금 차등이 추진되는 상황이다. 그런데 피지컬 AI 성공을 위해선 스마트폰 외 
로봇/자율차가 새로운 디바이스로 부각되는 가운데 5G/6G 네트워크 진화를 통해 AI 기
지국 확산이 필요하다. 따라서 국내에서도 요금 인상 명분은 충분해 보인다. 2026년 12월
엔 망 투자 촉진 방안이 발표될 예정이다. 국내에서도 미국처럼 통신 요금제 개편 이슈
가 커질 것이란 판단이다.

• LTE/5G 요금 통합으로 신규 요금제 출시 위한 사전 작업 완료 
• KT AI 결합 요금제 출시, 9만원 이상 AI 서비스 무료 제공 
• 5G SA/6G 투자 활성화 위해 통신사 요금제 개편 시도 예상

• ARPU 성장 기대감 높다는 점 감안 시 통신주 밸류에이션 매력적

### Page 5

3) 9월 통신업종 수익률은 KOSPI를 하회, 재료 부재한 가운데 주가 지지 부진 
9월 통신서비스 업종 수익률 
KOSPI를 하회, 통신 3사 모두 
주가 부진한 양상 지속

9월 국내 통신 서비스 업종 수익률은 KOSPI 수익률을 하회하였다. KOSPI가 IT 업종의 강
세로 상승 반전한 반면 통신서비스 업종은 시중 금리 상승으로 배당 매력도가 낮아지는 
가운데 2027년 서비스 매출액 성장 기대감이 아직 부각되지 못했기 때문이었다. 
종목별로는 2026년 주도주였던 SKT 주가가 하락한 가운데 KT와 LGU+의 주가도 부진한 
양상이었다. SKT는 엔트로픽 상장 및 실적 정상화 기대감이 기존 주가에 기반영된 측면이 
강하다는 인식과 더불어 데이터센터 매출 목표치가 과도하다는 인식으로 주가 상승이 나
타나지 못했다. LGU+는 자사주 매입 진행 중임에도 주가가 부진한 양상을 나타냈는데 자
사주 매입/소각이 장기적으로 지속될 수 있을 것인가에 대한 회의적인 시각 때문이었다. 
KT 역시 재료가 부족한 가운데 8월에 이어 9월에도 지지부진한 주가 흐름을 지속하였다.

도표 5. 2026년 9월 통신서비스 업종 수익률 점검 (단위: %, %p)

### Page 6

통신서비스 Overweight

하나증권•6

4) 강세장 아니라면 10월 통신주 시장 수익률 KOSPI를 상회할 전망 
10월 국내 통신 3사 주가 현재
보단 높아질 듯, 약세장 예상한
다면 비중확대 고려할 필요

2026년 10월 통신서비스 업종 수익률은 명목상 플러스 달성이 예상된다. 실적 시즌을 맞
이하여 양호한 실적을 달성할 것으로 예상되는 가운데 2027년으로 다가가면서 금리 하락
과 더불어 요금제 개편 기대감이 높아질 것이기 때문이다. 다소 이른 감이 있지만 2027
년으로 시각을 넘기면 오랜만에 통신서비스 서비스 매출액 성장 기대감이 높아질 수 있
어 현 주가 수준에서는 서서히 주가 반등이 나타날 것이란 판단이다. 따라서 강세장이 
아니라면 10월엔 통신서비스 업종 수익률이 시장 수익률을 상회할 것으로 판단한다. 
10월 통신 서비스 업종 이슈는 많지 않다. 1) 3분기 통신 3사 프리뷰 발표, 2) 미국 2027
년 하반기 이후 추가 주파수 공급 계획 발표, 3) 국내외 서비스 품질 차이에 따른 통신 
요금 차등 허용 논의 개시, 4) 상대적으로 적은 국내 5G 주파수 할당 폭 문제 논의, 5) 금
통위 10월 기준 금리 추가 인상 가능성 등이 대표적이다. 
전체적으로 볼 때 부정적인 이슈가 보이지 않는 가운데 장기 호재가 다수 눈에 뜨인다. 
물론 국내 기준 금리 추가 인상 가능성은 부담이다. 배당 수급이 적지 않은 통신주 매수
세를 위축시킬 수 있기 때문이다. 하지만 10월 한 차례를 끝으로 기준 금리 인상이 마침
표를 찍고 시중 금리가 연말부터 내려갈 수 있다는 점은 오히려 장기적으로 볼 때 통신
주 매수세를 자극할 수 있을 것으로 판단한다. 금리 상승 부담은 10월을 정점으로 낮아
질 것이란 판단이다. 
반면 3분기 실적이 전년동기비 개선되고 2027년 이동전화 서비스 매출액 기대감이 높아
질 수 있다는 점은 긍정적으로 평가된다. 특히 전세계적으로 단순 데이터 사용량 차이가 
아닌 서비스 품질 차이에 따른 요금 차등 문제가 이슈화되고 있다는 점은 고무적이다. 
5G SA 서비스에 높은 요금을 적용할 수 있기 때문이다. 국내 5G 총 주파수 할당 폭이 미
국/중국대비 크게 적다는 점 역시 요금제 개편 이슈를 불러올 수 있어 호재라는 판단이
다. 2027년 신규 5G 주파수 할당이 국내에도 이루어지면 망 투자 부담 증가를 빌미로 당
연히 요금제 개편이 이루어질 것이기 때문이다. 해킹 이슈, 명예 퇴직금 지급으로 2025
년 3분기 통신사 실적이 좋지 않았음을 감안하면 실적 시즌 양호한 주가 흐름이 나타날 
수 있어 장/단기 통신주 투자 유망하다는 판단이다.

도표 7. 10월 통신서비스 업종 이슈 점검 
3분기 실적 전년동기비 크게 개
선되는 가운데 5G 요금제 개편 
기대감 높아질 수 있어 금리 상
승 종료되면 통신주 오를 것

구분 내용 
긍정적 요소 
• 미국 어퍼 C밴드 주파수 경매 확정, 2027년 4월 경매 예정 
• 상대적으로 적은 국내 5G 주파수 할당 폭 이슈화되는 양상, 2027년 주파수 경매 가능성 높아 
• 서비스 품질별 통신 요금 차이 허용 이슈로 부상 중, 5G SA 요금 인상 가능성 고조 
• 3분기 프리뷰 본격화, 낮은 기저로 2026년 3분기 통신사 높은 이익 성장 전망 
• 통신 3사/과기부 AI 네트워크 민관 협의체 구성, 12월 망 투자 촉진 정책 발표 예상 
• SKT/LGU+ 5G SA 연내 상용화 예정 언급, 2026년 연말 요금제 개편 논의 예상 
• KT 9만원 이상 고가 요금제에 AI 서비스 무료 제공, 통신사 AI 과금화 본격화 
• 올 가을 국내 독자 파운데이션 AI 모델 사업자 발표 예정, SKT 선정 가능성 높아 
• LTE/5G 요금제 통합, 요금 인하 정책 사실상 마무리된 것으로 판단 
• SK하이퍼 SPC 형태로 설립, 데이터센터 이익 SKT 배당금으로 유입될 것 
• LGU+ 자사주 900억원 취득 중, 2027년 소각으로 장기 주주 환원 증대 이루어질 전망 
• 기저 효과로 2026년 SKT/LGU+ 높은 이익 성장 달성 전망 
• 피지컬 AI 경쟁 심화, 국내에서도 네트워크 고도화 전략 추진 본격화 전망 
• 미국을 중심으로 양자암호통신 급부상, 차세대 서비스에 기본 탑재/킬러 서비스 역할 수행할 듯 
• 제조사간 경쟁 어려워 단통법 폐지에도 국내 통신 유통 시장 크게 영향 받긴 어려운 상황 
• 규제 당국 추진에도 제 4 이동통신사업자 선정 가능성 낮음, 재무적 이슈 여전히 크기 때문 
부정적 요소 
• 시중 금리 상승세, 기준 금리 추가 인상 가능성 높아 
• 이동전화매출액 정체 양상, 비용 통제로 이익 증가하는 전형적인 성숙기 후반 모습 연출 중 
• KT 토탈 영업 TF로 배치된 2, 300명 본업 복귀, 2026년 이후 인건비 다시 증가 예상 
• LGU+ 자사주 소각 규모 감소로 2026년 연간 주주이익환원 규모 일시적 감소 예상 
• SKT를 제외한 KT/LGU+ 2026년 배당금 총액 정체 예상 
• 데이터센터 투자 증대로 단기 현금흐름 악화 예상 
• 2024년 이후 트래픽 정체 양상 심각, 요금제 개편 없을 시 통신사 매출 정체 우려감 커질 것 
• 통신사+금융권 합산 알뜰폰 M/S 60%로 제한 전망, MVNO 가입자 확대에 제동 걸릴 전망 
• 국내 통신사 PBR 낮지만 해외 통신사 대비 ROE가 낮은 편 
종합 평가 • AI 기지국/5G SA 도입 기대감 팽창 국면, 금리 하락 반전 시 통신주 오를 것 
 자료: 하나증권

2. 12개월 Top Pick을 SKT로 유지, 10월 투자매력도는 SKT>KT=LGU+ 순으로 평가 
  1) 12개월 Top Pick을 SKT로 유지 
12개월 Top Pick을  
SKT로 유지,  
2026년 이익 급성장 속에 DPS 
정상화, 2027년 AI 데이터센터/
기지국 사업 기대감 상승 예상되
기 때문 
 통신서비스업종 12개월 Top Pick을 SKT로 유지한다. 1) 장/단기 이익 흐름 및 성장률, 2) 
장/단기 배당 또는 주주이익환원 성장률, 3) 기대 배당 수익률 및 주주환원 수익률, 4) 네
트워크 경쟁력 및 차세대 서비스 도입에 따른 수혜 정도, 5) M/S 추이 및 매출액 성장률, 
6) 마케팅비용/감가상각비/경비 등 비용 전망, 7) 정부 규제 영향, 8) 유통 경쟁력 및 유통 
구조 변화에 따른 영향, 9) 신사업 및 M&A가 실적 및 주가에 미치는 영향, 10) 자회사 실
적 및 IPO 추진 예상, 11) 지배구조 개편 가능성 및 영향 등을 종합적으로 고려한 결과다. 
이러한 기준 하에 향후 12개월간 투자 매력도를 평가하면 통신 3사 중 SKT가 가장 투자 
유망하다는 판단이다. 
무엇보다 SKT를 12개월 장기 Top Pick으로 제시하는 이유는 2025년과는 달리 2026년도
에는 통신 3사 중 가장 높은 이익 성장이 유력한 가운데 DPS 정상화가 예상되기 때문이
다. 5G 신규 요금제 출시 논의가 시작되는 가운데 장기 DPS 성장 기대감이 높아질 것이
라 여전히 기대배당수익률은 매력적이란 판단이다. 장기적으로 2027년에는 비과세 배당
이 가능하다는 점에서 더욱 그렇다. 올해 국책 AI 사업자로 선정될 가능성이 높고 AI 기
지국 활성화와 더불어 2027년 5G SA 도입 기대감이 높다는 점을 감안하면 장기 주가 우
상향 기조가 예상된다.

도표 34. 통신 3사 투자 환경상 긍정적/부정적 요인 점검 
구분 긍정적 요인 부정적 요인 
SKT 
• AI 데이터센터/기지국 수익 증대 본격화 
• 26년 4분기 비과세 배당 실시, 엔트로픽 차익 발생 기대 
• 데이터센터 이익 발생이 장기 배당금 증가로 이어질 것 
• 현금흐름 개선, 2026년도엔 DPS 급증 예상 
• 휴대폰 가입자 감소, M/S 하락 양상 
• 5G 가입자 정체/이동전화매출액 정체 양상 
• 5G SA로의 진화, 신규 요금제 출시 지연 
• 배당 성향 50% 상회, 이익 증가 나와야 장기 배당 성장 가능 
KT 
• 매출 성장 기반으로 장기 본사 이익 성장 지속될 전망 
• 부동산 분양 이익 발생으로 자회사 실적 호전 지속 전망 
• AI 결합 상품 출시 통한 과금화에 나서는 양상 
• 낮은 PBR 개선시키기 위한 정책 나올 수 있는 상황 
• 구조조정효과 미흡, 2026년 이후 인건비 다시 증가 전환 예상 
• 자사주 배당 전환 당분간 어려울 것 
• 인당 효율성 낮음, 과다한 영업비용 존재 
• 외국인 한도 소진, 수급상 불리함 존재 
LGU+ 
• 이익 성장률/배당 성향 수준으로 볼 때 장기 배당 증가 여력 존재 
• 비용 이슈 종결, 이익 측면에서 양호한 흐름으로 전환 예상 
• 2026년 높은 연결 영업이익 성장 기록할 전망 
• 통신 3사 중 PER, PBR 가장 낮음, 상대 서비스 매출액 성장률 높음 
• 이동전화매출액 성장 둔화, B2B 매출 성장 폭 둔화 
• 과거 대비 낮은 5G 순증 가입자 M/S 기록 중 
• 주주 환원 규모로 보면 2026년 단기 감소 예상 
• 2026년 총 배당금 규모 정체 예상 
종합평가 • 2026년 이익 급증, DPS 정상화 가능성, AI 기지국/데이터센터 대표 주자임을 감안하여 SKT를 12개월 최선호주로 선정 
자료: 하나증권

### Page 14

통신서비스 Overweight

하나증권•14

2) 10월 매력도는 SKT>KT=LGU+ 순, SKT 올라야 KT/LGU+ 주가 상승 전망 
10월 종목별 투자 매력도는  
SKT>KT=LGU+ 순으로 평가, 
그나마 주가 상승 재료 있는 
SKT를 Top Pick으로 제시

10월 통신서비스 업종 내 종목별 투자 매력도를 SKT>KT=LGU+ 순으로 제시한다. 10월에
도 통신서비스 월간 Top Pick은 SKT로 선정하는데 3분기 실적이 양호할 것으로 전망되는 
가운데 엔트로픽 IPO, 국책 AI 사업자 선정, AI 기지국 투자 본격화 등이 기대되기 때문이
다.  
10월에도 SKT 주가 전망을 긍정적으로 유지하는 이유는 다음과 같다. 양호한 실적/현금 
흐름을 바탕으로 비과세 배당이 실시될 4분기 이후 세후 배당금이 증가할 수 있으며 AI 
사업 부문에서 데이터센터와 기지국을 바탕으로 실체를 가장 잘 입증할 것으로 판단되기 
때문이다. 2026년 연간 SKT 연결 영업이익은 당초 우려와 달리 2조원에 육박할 전망이며 
엔트로픽 평가 차익 역시 3조원~7조원에 달할 것으로 보인다. 따라서 데이터센터와 기지
국 투자 증가에도 불구하고 2026년 총 배당금은 2024년 수준으로 회복될 전망이다. 4분
기 세후 배당금은 1,050원이 예상된다. 여기에 AI 사업이 기지국과 데이터센터 고도화로 
실체를 드러낼 전망이다. AI 기지국은 본사 이동전화 매출액 증가를 이끌 것이며 SK하이
퍼와 SK호라이즌은 데이터센터 발생 이익을 SKT 배당금 증대로 이끌 전망이다. 현 주가 
수준에선 장/단기 투자 매력도가 높아 보인다. 
KT의 경우엔 10월에도 특별한 호재가 있는 것은 아니다. 하지만 가격 메리트를 기반으로 
SKT 주가 상승이 나타난다면 주가 반등에 동참할 것으로 보인다. 따라서 2027년까지 내
다본 장기 매수를 추천한다. 5만원 초중반에서의 매수는 여전히 무리가 없다는 판단이다. 
연말 이후 시중 금리 하락과 더불어 규제 기관과 통신사간 5G SA 도입 및 신규 주파수 
할당 논의가 시작될 경우 낮은 주가를 기반으로 주가 상승이 나타날 수 있기 때문이다. 
사실상 2026년 KT 주식 매수의 최대 고민은 배당/주주환원이 증가하기 어렵고 높은 기
저로 영업이익 감소가 불가피하다는 것이었다. 그런데 3분기 프리뷰가 끝나가는 10월 하
순부터는 대다수 투자가들이 이를 인지했을 가능성이 높아 더 이상 악재가 될 수 없다는 
판단이다. 악재가 대부분 주가에 반영된 상태에서 2027년 이후 다시 이동전화매출액 증
가 기대감이 높아질 수 있다고 보면 시중 금리 하락 반전 시 4.6%에 달하는 기대배당수
익률은 충분히 매력적으로 부각될 수 있다는 판단이다. 
LGU+의 경우에는 예상과 달리 주가 부진이 지속되는 양상이다. 아직은 장기 주주환원증
대에 대한 투자가들의 신뢰가 약하기 때문이다. 자사주 매입/소각분 감소로 2026년 총 
주주환원 감소가 불가피한 상황이며 아직은 2027년 주주환원 증대에 대한 확신이 크지 
않은 편이다. 더불어 2028년 이후 자사주 매입/소각이 지속될 것에 대한 신뢰도 그리 높
지 않은 점도 주가 상승의 제약 요인이 되고 있다. 하지만 가격 메리트와 배당 매력도가 
부각될 수 있는 상황이므로 15,000원 수준에서는 매수로 임할 것을 권한다. 시중 금리가 
다시 하락해 회사채 발행 금리와 배당수익률간 차이가 벌어질 경우 자사주 매입을 통한 
배당 지급 주식 수 감소 정책이 지속될 것이기 때문이다. 서서히 연말로 다가가면서 
2027년 실적 및 주주환원으로 투자가들의 시각이 이동할 것이다. 그런 관점에서 보면 
LGU+의 투자 매력도가 높아질 수 있다. 연말부터 시중 금리 하락이 나타난다면 LGU+의 
기대배당수익률이 충분히 부각될 수 있을 것이란 판단이다.

 당사는 2026년 9월 30일 현재 해당회사의 지분을 1%이상 보유 하고 있지 않습니
다  
 본 자료를 작성한 애널리스트(김홍식)는 자료의 작성과 관련하여 외부의 압력이나 
부당한 간섭을 받지 않았으며, 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 신의성실 하게 
작성하였습니다. 
 본 자료는 기관투자가 등 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
 본 자료를 작성한 애널리스트(김홍식)는 2026년 9월 30일 현재 해당회사의 유가증
권을 보유하고 있지 않습니다

 투자의견의 유효기간은 추천일 이후 12개월을 기준으로 적용 
 기업의 분류 
BUY(매수)_목표주가가 현주가 대비 15% 이상 상승 여력 
Neutral(중립)_목표주가가 현주가 대비 -15%~15% 등락 
Reduce(비중축소)_목표주가가 현주가 대비 -15% 이상 하락 가능 
 산업의 분류 
Overweight(비중확대)_업종지수가 현재지수 대비 15% 이상 상승 여력 
Neutral(중립)_업종지수가 현재지수 대비 -15%~15% 등락 
Underweight(비중축소)_업종지수가 현재지수 대비 -15% 이상 하락 가능 
투자등급 BUY(매수) Neutral(중립) Reduce(매도) 합계 
금융투자상품의 비율 98.18% 1.82% 0.00% 100% 
* 기준일: 2026년 09월 27일

도 무단 복제 및 배포 될 수 없습니다. 또한 본 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사가 신뢰
할 만한 자료 및 정보로 얻어진 것이나, 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없으므
로 투자자 자신의 판단과 책임하에 최종결정을 하시기 바랍니다. 따라서 어떠한 경
우에도 본 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재의 증빙자료로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

Document 546: [產業] 마약 예방도 관리가 우선이다! - 유안타증권

Source PDF: all report pdf/[產業] 마약 예방도 관리가 우선이다! - 유안타증권.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

마약 투약 추정 인구 65만명?  
최근 마약과 관련된 뉴스를 자주 접할 수 있다. 드라마, 영화의 소재로도 자주 사용된다. 지
난 7월 tvN 프로그램인 유퀴즈 온더 블록에 마약범죄수사계 형사 손희민 경감이 출연, 국내 
마약 투약 추정인구가 65만명이라고 언급, 충격을 주었다. 마약이 확대되는 이유로 지목되
는 것은 ①낮아진 가격, ②용이한 접근성 등이다.  
마약사범 
대검찰청에서 발표하는 마약류 범죄백서에 따르면, 2025년 마약사범(마약, 대마, 향정신성의
약품)은 2만 3,403명으로 전년대비 1.7% 증가했다. 5년전인 2020년(1만 8,050명)과 비교
하면 29.7% 증가한 수치이며, 10년전인 2015년(1만 1,916명)대비 거의 두배인 96.4% 증
가한 수치이다.  
세부적으로 살펴보면 마약, 대마, 향정신성의약품 중 향정신성의약품 관련 마약사범이 가장 
많다. 비중도 상향되고 있다. 2005년 74.8%, 2010년 69.6%, 2015년 80.8%, 2020년 
70.0%, 2025년 82.1%로 Up&Down은 있지만 우상향하고 있다. 우려가 되는 사항은 마약
의 중독성, 내성, 금단증상 등을 고려시 지속적인 미약복용 가능성이 높을 것으로 추정되는 
20~30대의 비중이 높다는 점이 2025년 기준 20~30대의 마약사범의 비중은 전체의 절반
이 넘는 59.4%이다. 2024년도에는 60.8%로 유사한 수치이다. 청소년 마약류 사범도 늘고 
있다. 법무부에 따르면 올해 상반기 19세 이하 마약류 사범 비중은 전체의 4.1%(456명)으
로 전년대비 1.2% 증가했다고 언급했다.  
법무부에 따르면 마약 중독이 가져오는 1인당 사회적 비용은 10억 2,300여만원으로 진료
비 등 의료비 800여만원+실직 등에 따른 생산성 손실 비용 6.6억원+형사 사법비용 5,400
만원+사망ㆍ중독 유발 등 주변에 주는 고통 비용 3.54억원을 제시했다. 2025년 2.3만명을 
대입하면 23.9조원의 사회적 비용이 발생한다는 것을 의미한다. 
관련기업 
식품의약품안전처가 제출한 자료에 따르면 최근 5년간(2022년~2026.8월) 의료기관과 약국 
등 마약류 취급 현장에서 도난ㆍ분실된 마약류가 6만 7,879개이다. 도난ㆍ분신된 마약률가 
회수되지 못할 경우 불법 유통으로 이어질 가능성이 높다는 점에서 대책이 필요한 상황이
다. 제이브이엠(054950)의 마약류 의약품 전용 보관ㆍ관리할 수 있는 마약보관함을 지난 6
월에 출시했다. 이중 잠금장치로 되어 있으며 실시간 불출 및 재고관리가 가능한 제품이라
는 점에서 병원/약국에서의 적용 확대가 기대된다.  
마약여부를 확인하기 위해 진단기기 및 진단키트에 대한 관심도 확대될 수 있을 것으로 기
대된다. 의료기기안심책방 의료기기통합정보시스템내 품목허가를 받은 제품을 보유한 기업
은 바디텍메드(206640)등이 있다.

종목 투자의견 목표주가 (원) 
제이브이엠 Not Rated (M) - (M) 
바디텍메드 Not Rated (I) - (I)

마약 예방도 관리가 우선이다! 
Sector Report  2026.09.30

스몰캡

### Page 2

2
스몰캡
마약
마약(Narcotics)는 무감각 또는 감각 둔화를 뜻하는 그리스어에서 유래한 말로, 일반적으로 진통, 진정,
수면 등의 약리작용을 나타내고 의존성 및 남용 가능성이 있어, 오ㆍ남용할 경우 심각한 보건상ㆍ사회
적 폐해를 초래할 수 있는 약물을 의미한다.
WTO에서는 마약류를 약물사용의 욕구가 강해 강제에 이를 정도로 강하고(의존성), 사용 약물의 양이
증가하는 경향이 있으며(내성), 사용중단시 온몸에 견디기 힘든 증상이 나타나며(금단증상), 개인에 한
정되지 아니하고 사회에도 해를 끼치는 약물로 정의하고 있다.
법적으로 마약류는 마약류 관리에 관한 법률로 마약, 대마, 향정신성의약품으로 분리하고 있다. 일반적
으로는 인간의 중추신경계에 영향을 미쳐 중추신경의 작용을 과도하게 하거나 억제하는 물질 중 신체적
ㆍ정신적 의존성이 있는 것으로서 관련 법규에 따라 규제 대상으로 지정되어 관리되고 있는 물질을 의

미한다.
마약은 생약에서 추출한 천연마약, 추출 알칼로이드, 이를 원료로 화학적으로 합성한 합성마약으로 분
류한다. 천연마약으로는 양귀비, 아편 등이 있다. 추출 알칼로이드에는 모르핀, 코데인, 헤로인, 코카인
등이 있다. 합성마약으로는 페티닌계, 메타톤계, 펜타닐 등이 있다.
향정신성의약품은 인간의 중추신경계에 작용하여 각성ㆍ진통 등의 효과를 목적으로 개발ㆍ사용된 약물
을 의미한다. 그러나 투여시 의존성과 중독성을 유발하여 오ㆍ남용할 경우 인체에 심각한 악영향을 끼
칠 우려가 있어 마약류 관리에 관한 법률에 근거하여 지정ㆍ관리되고 잇는 규제 대상 물질이다. 종류로
는 LSD, 합성대마, 메스케티논 및 그 유사체, 크라톰, 메트암페타민, MDMA, 케타민, 야바, 메틸페니
데이트, 날부핀, 덱스트로메트로판, 펜플루라민, GHB, 프로포폴, 에토미데이트 등이 있다.
대마는 칸나비스속에 속하는 한해살이 식물로, 식물분류상 삼과에 속하는 식물이다. 우리나라에는 예로

부터 삼으로 불렸고, 한자로 대마로 표기된다. 국내에서는 대마가 전통적으로 섬유용 작물로 재배되어
왔으나, 흡연 목적의 대마초 남용은 1960년대 후반 주한미군 기지촌 등을 중심으로 확산된 것으로 알
려져 있다. 종류로는 대마초, 해시시 등이 있다.

키움증권 리서치센터 투자전략팀

Compliance Notice 
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 동 자료에 게시된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 
작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
고지사항 
본 조사분석자료는 당사의 리서치센터가 신뢰할 수 있는 자료 및 정보로부터 얻은 것이나, 당사가 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없고, 통지 없이 의견이 변경될 수 있습니다. 
조사분석자료는 유가증권 투자를 위한 정보제공을 목적으로 당사 고객에게 배포되는 참고자료로서, 유가증권의 종류, 종목, 매매의 구분과 방법 등에 관한 의사결정은 전적으로 투자자 자신의 
판단과 책임하에 이루어져야 하며, 당사는 본 자료의 내용에 의거하여 행해진 일제의 투자행위 결과에 대하여 어떠한 책임도 지지 않으며 법적 분쟁에서 증거로 사용 될 수 없습니다.  
조사 분석자료를 무단으로 인용, 복제, 전시, 배포, 전송, 편집, 번역, 출판하는 등의 방법으로 저작권을 침해하는 경우에는 관련법에 의하여 민형사상 책임을 지게 됩니다.

미 국채금리, 상승 속도에 주목

다음 주에도 중동발 지정학적 리스크에 따른 국제유가 흐름과 연준위원들의 발언, 9 월 FOMC 의사록에 
주목할 필요가 있다. 모두 최근 미국 시장금리의 방향과 변동성에 직접적인 영향을 줄 수 있는 
변수들이기 때문이다. 우선 국제유가가 안정되거나 추가 하락할 경우 최근 인플레이션 우려와 함께 
가파르게 상승했던 미 국채금리도 상승세가 다소 진정될 여지가 있다. 여기에 전일 제퍼슨 연준 
부의장의 다소 비둘기적인 발언이 금리 하락으로 이어졌던 만큼, 향후 연준위원들이 추가 금리 인상 
필요성은 인정하면서도 시점이나 속도에 여유를 두는 모습을 보인다면 시장의 긴축 경계를 낮추며 금리 
상승 압력을 완화할 수 있다. 다만 다음주 공개되는 9 월 FOMC 의사록 자체는 상대적으로 매파적으로 
해석될 가능성이 있다. 당시 연준은 기준금리를 25bp 인상했고, 결정도 만장일치였다. 특히 성명에서 
여전히 높은 인플레이션에 대한 경계감을 강조했던 만큼, 회의 내부에서도 물가 안정 필요성에 상당한 
무게가 실렸을 가능성이 높다. 결국 최근 미 국채금리 상승은 단순히 연준의 추가 긴축 우려만으로 
설명하기 어렵다. 미국 경제지표의 견조함과 인플레이션 경계, 재정건전성 우려에 따른 장기물 기간 
프리미엄 상승이 복합적으로 작용하고 있기 때문이다. 이 세 요인이 단기간에 모두 해소되기는 쉽지 
않아 금리가 추세적으로 크게 하락하기 위한 여건은 아직 충분하지 않다. 따라서 당장은 금리의 방향 
전환보다는 상승 속도가 진정될 수 있는지를 확인하는 것이 중요하다. 이를 위해서는 우선 국제유가의 
안정이 필요하며, 동시에 연준위원들의 발언이 추가 금리 인상에 대한 강한 경계에서 한발 물러나는 
모습을 보여줄 필요가 있다. 유가 안정과 연준의 덜 매파적인 커뮤니케이션이 동시에 나타날 경우 최근 
가팔랐던 미 국채금리 상승세가 일단 진정될 가능성이 높아 보인다.

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.

strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly

diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge

strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.

Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.
The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Iran Tehran's access to global finance Maximum financial pressure Sanctions, asset restrictions, dollar-system leverage
Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product
1 BloombergNEF , Data Centers on Track to Suck Up a Fifth of US Power Use by 2035, July 21, 2026.
2 BloombergNEF , AI Buildout Drives 76% Power Bill Jump on Largest US Grid, May 14, 2026.
3 Data Center Watch, Q1 2026 Insights Report, May 2026.

4 Pictet Asset Management, “Sizing AI’s Environmental Footprint,” July 2026.
5 International Energy Agency, as of January 2026.
6 Google Cloud, Amin Vahdat and Jeff Dean, “How Much Energy Does Google’s AI Use? We Did the Math,” August 21, 2025.
7 U.S. Environmental Protection Agency, WaterSense, May 2026.
8 Pictet Asset Management, “Sizing AI’s Environmental Footprint,” July 2026.
Investment Implications
Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book 1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings hav e generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than tw o-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.” 2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market. 2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of inv estment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractiv e in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market mo ves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting go vernment
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff
Source: Chief Investment Office. Data as of October 22, 2026.
* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of Bank of America Corporation.

of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

Earnings above all 
• Higher oil prices are in a tussle with stronger growth. We expect strong growth 
to help the global economy weather inflation risks, allowing equities to focus on the 
strength of earnings growth. Oil prices remain the key risk to monitor 
• Maintain a preference for equities over bonds and cash.  Strong earnings 
growth means we would use the recent pause to add exposure to our preferred 
markets and themes. The US and Asia ex-Japan remain our preferred regions 
• Take advantage of higher bond yields via credit risk but cap duration. The 
risk of higher bond yields looks most pressing in ultra -long maturities, but credit 
quality remains strong. We are Overweight EM USD bonds 
Hits from the 1990s 
Rising bond yields have been a key market theme.  Since mid -year, 10 -year 
government bond yields have risen approximately 40-80bps across the US, Japan and 
Euro area. Against this backdrop, gold has delivered high single -digit returns, while 
global equities have delivered low single-digit returns. 
Higher oil prices are in a tussle with robust growth data . The lack of progress 
towards a negotiated settlement in the US -Iran conflict and rising fears of shipping 
restrictions extending to the Bab al-Mandab Strait have meant that oil prices are once 
again testing the USD 100/bbl level. These worries have been exacerbated by 
increasing worries about refined product (eg. diesel, jet fuel, etc) supply. Despite these 
concerns, US growth data has held up remarkably well, as illustrated by the latest PMI 
and retail sales data. This is underpinned by AI investment growth.  
Real, rather than nominal, energy prices are key.  A chart of inflation -adjusted 
energy prices, though, illustrates why the global economy has managed to hold up 
despite these risks. Oil prices in real (inflation-adjusted) terms are closer to long term 
averages rather than at true ‘oil shock’ levels. This, together with the global economy’s 
lower oil intensity relative to history, is why we expect  growth and inflation to avoid 
worst-case scenarios as long as nominal oil prices avoid an excursion into the USD 
120-150/bbl range.

The Fed is likely to hike twice more by mid -2027. The 
acceleration in AI-driven investment growth means we believe 
the chances of a shift from our core soft-landing to no-landing 
economic scenario have gone up. This, combined with still -
high inflation readings mean we now expect the Fed to hike 
rates twice more by mid -2027. In contrast, the BoJ and ECB 
are expected to hike thrice and once more respectively. 
Buy-the-dip in equities 
1990s rate hikes a better parallel then 2022 . There is 
understandably some concern about whether rising central 
bank policy rates, and rising bond yields across maturities, will 
present a challenge to equity markets. However, we believe 
rate hikes in the 1990s are a better historical parallel, when 
central banks were hiking rates into strong economic and 
earnings growth, resulting in equity markets maintaining their 
pace of gains. The 2022 hiking cycle, in contrast, represented 
an inflation surprise, which we believe is not the case today. 
Strong earnings growth overwhelms other factors today. 
Still-strong economic and earnings growth means that we 
remain constructive on the outlook for equities and other risky 
assets going into year -end and beyond. The pause over the 
last couple of months was likely driven by a combination or 
rising bond yields , September seasonality and excessive 
investor optimism around mid-year.  
However, earnings expectations for global equities remain 
very strong (especially in our preferred US and Asia ex-Japan 
regional markets). We believe these earnings remain 
sufficiently robust to outweigh the drag from higher bond 
yields. Therefore, w e remain Overweight equities relative to 
bonds and cash, though we believe it is prudent to ensure the 
size of the equities Overweight is not excessive. Within Asia, 
we also trim China equities to a core holding, leaving Taiwan 
as our only regional Overwei ght, given the ongoing focus on 
AI investment-driven markets for now. 
Bumps remain part of the journey. As we laid out in our mid-
year outlook, the second half of this year is expected to be 
characterised by positive, but bumpy returns as a result of the 
factors laid out in the previous section, but also US mid -term 
election risks. The second half has thus far played out as 
expected given sufficient bumps, but still robust earnings 
growth. We would not be surprised by some volatility around 
US mid-term elections, but any further pullbacks or pauses 
would be opportunit ies to raise exposure given strong 
earnings growth is expected to ultimately dominate. 
Prefer credit over duration 
Corporate credit quality remains strong . Aggregate 
indicators of credit quality suggest credit quality remains 
strong across both Investment Grade and High Yield bonds 
across most regions. US IG bond yields face some upward 
pressure from significant hyperscaler supply, but supply -
driven spread widening tends to be relatively short -lived in 
nature and should not signal significant credit quality worries. 
Longer-maturity bonds remain at risk of further volatility. 
In contrast, we continue to be concerned that the volatility in 
very long maturity bonds could extend. Continued inflation 
and debt worries mean it is possible investors continue to 
demand a greater ‘term premium’ (ie. a premium for holding 
bonds with very  long maturities). We continue to believe 
limiting bond duration to 3-7 years remains appropriate.  
We continue to prefer  corporate and EM over government 
bonds. This implies earning a higher yield by taking on more 
credit risk (via corporate or EM bonds) rather than adding 
more duration risk. Within bonds, we remain Overweight EM 
USD bonds and view IG and HY bonds as core holdings. 
Modest USD weakness; gold to grind higher 
USD bounce led by pricing of Fed hikes . However, we 
believe Fed hikes are now excessively priced. At the same 
time, we see room for non-US rates to rise further, especially 
in Japan where the undervalued JPY is likely to strengthen. 
This is why modest USD weakness is likely to resume. 
Gold to continue grinding higher. While the move higher is 
likely to be characteri sed by two -steps-forward-one-step-
back, we believe an ultimately softer USD and continued EM 
central bank demand should result in continued gains in the 
precious metal over both the next 3- and 12-month periods.

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): We have slightly reduced the probability of a soft landing from 50% by raising 
the odds of a ‘no -landing’. The global economy has  picked up steam this summer, powered by AI investments in the US and 
Asia. This is reflected in robust upgrades to corporate earnings estimates  and PMIs. Fiscal easing in Germany and Japan is 
supporting growth. Meanwhile, inflation has picked up amid higher energy prices due to a resurgen t Middle East conflict. As a 
result, the Fed turned hawkish, hiking rates for the first time since 2023. It was joined by the ECB and BoJ – the first time all 
three major central banks hiked rates in the same month. We expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the 
BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, disinflation should return by next year as energy and tariff effects fade, enabling 
the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. Meanwhile, China is likely to accelerate fiscal spending to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We raised the probability from 25% amid an acceleration in the AI-driven investment 
growth. If oil prices ease, US tax cuts, an AI -fuelled stock market boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan could boost 
“animal spirits” further. A global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also spur global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): This tail risk scenario includes 15% chance of a stagflation unfolding if geopolitical conflicts 
worsen and oil stays above USD 120/bbl. We also assign a 10% chance to a recession, potentially caused by a more rapid pace 
of rate hikes to quell inflation, a stock market downturn negating the wealth effect, or a bond selloff due to fiscal concerns.

Policy rates watch  
Fed turns hawkish to build credibility as oil reignites 
inflation; likely to hike twice more by June 2027. The US 
economy appears resilient, with consensus estimates for 
2026 GDP clustering around 2.1% for both 2026 and 2027. 
The main engine is the AI investment boom and hyperscaler 
datacentre buildout, reinforced by easy financial conditions 
and healthy private-sector balance sheets. The robust outlook 
was reflected in August’s PMI data, with both manufacturing 
and service sector confidence beating expectations. 
The labour market has picked up  this year , with average 
monthly payrolls of 80 ,000 vs. 10 ,000 in 2025 . The 
unemployment rate at 4.1%  suggests full employment, and 
the workweek lengthened in August. Offsetting vulnerabilities 
include a low savings rate, fading fiscal support, soft real  
personal spending and oil prices close to USD 100/bbl. 
Inflation remains a challenge. Core PCE inflation has run 
above 3% every month since March, and the hawkish case 
rests on supercore inflation remaining around 3 %, raising 
concerns about energy cost pass-through. Nevertheless, 
August's firm inflation print came from airfares, hotels and a 
record jump in wireless plans  (which is idiosyncratic) , while 
shelter cooled and core goods rose just 0. 7% y/y. Core 
consumer inflation at 2. 4% y/y is the lowest since 2021, and 
BEA methodology changes in end-September should 
mechanically shave 0.2-0.3pp off historical core PCE inflation. 
Following the unanimous Fed vote to hike in September, and 
still elevated inflation, we pencil in one more rate hike this 
year, followed by another 25bps hike to 4.5% by June 2027. 
This pace is slower than market pricing of another c. 90bps of 
hikes in the next 12 months. However, we expect disinflation 
to resume next year as the impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. 
This should create space for the Fed to cut rates by Q3 2027.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more: Euro area activity has proved more 
resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 growth estimate 
has been revised up to 0.9%, from 0.5% as recently as July, 
with ECB staff projections upgraded to  1.4% for 2027 and 
1.5% for 2028. Manufacturing PMIs have improved to 52.7 
regionally and services PMI to 53.0, helped by German fiscal 
delivery and falling household savings rates. A softer Q3 looks 
weather-driven, due to heatwaves and low Rhine water levels, 
though higher energy prices and real yields still cap demand. 
Headline inflation hit a near three -year high of 3.3% y/y in 
August and may peak around 3.4 -3.6% in Q4 2026 before 
easing through 2027. Underlying pressures look calmer: core 
inflation drifted down to 2.4%, services eased, and negotiated 
wage growth slowed to 2.4% y/y in Q2, down from a peak of 
5.6% in 2024 . Long -term inflation expectations remain 
anchored with no evidence of second -round effects. Inflation 
risks are two -sided – oil above USD 100/bbl and gas above 
EUR 70/MWh are fuelling near -term inflation, which in turn 
should hurt demand, driving inflation below 2% by H2 2027. 
The ECB raised its deposit rate to 2.50% in September, the 
top of neutral estimates. We see scope for one more rate hike 
to 2.75% by year -end before a pause in H1 2027. As such 
pricing of 95bps more tightening toward an above-3% terminal 
rate appears too hawkish. We expect easing of energy-driven 
price pressures to allow for ECB rate cuts by Q3 2027. 
China to boost fiscal spending to support growth. China 
is entering a stabilisation phase in which production is firming, 
but the recovery remains narrow. The official manufacturing 
PMI rose to 49.8 in August from 49.2, with new orders, export 
orders and production back in expansionary territory, while 
export growth accelerated to 25.0% y/y. Momentum, however, 
is concentrated in high -tech and export manufacturing: retail 
sales, fixed-asset investment and credit growth remain soft, 
and the property downturn has yet to bottom. Full -year 2026 
growth should land near 4.5 –4.6%, broadly in line with the 
official 4.5–5% target, as domestic demand stays weak. 
Reflation is under way, but shallow and cost-driven rather than 
demand-led. Headline consumer inflation edged up to 0.8% 
y/y in August and producer inflation to 3.8%, lifted by higher 
commodity and energy prices and by official efforts to curb 
excessive price-cutting. Full-year inflation is expected around 
0.8–1.0%, against roughly zero in 2025, with producer 
inflation swinging from −2.6% to about +2.5%. The persistent 
supply-demand imbalance should cap the reflation impulse. 
Policy settings remain supportive, but with no rush to 
stimulate. Fiscal policy takes the primary role via faster 
spending and utilisation of bond proceeds, with broad fiscal 
spending potentially accelerating in H2 versus a sharp 
contraction in H1, and the augmented fiscal deficit widening 
modestly. Monetary policy plays a complementary role: a 
25bps cut in bank reserve requirements is likely, while the 
policy rate should stay unchanged. Slow public financing 
raises the likelihood of further policy fine-tuning this autumn. 
BoJ to hike once a quarter until Q2 2027. Japan's economy 
is proving more resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 
growth estimate has been revised up over the past month to 
0.8% from 0.6%. Exports rose 19% y/y in August, a twelfth 
consecutive y/y growth, while core machinery and machine 
tool orders indicate higher borrowing costs have yet to dent 
corporate investment appetite. Real wages climbed 2.4% y/y 
in July, the strongest since May 2021. The weak link is the 
household sector: spending continued to contract y/y, leaving 
an unusual two -speed economy in which external and 
corporate demand carry growth while domestic demand lags. 
Underlying inflation sits close to the 2% target, with pressure 
building upstream rather than from demand. Energy and 
commodity prices have risen sharply and earlier yen 
weakness has amplified import costs, with producer inflation 
running well ahead of CPI . The 10-year breakeven inflation 
has risen close to May’s record high of 2.3% amid growing risk 
of pass-through from producer to consumer prices.  
Bank of Japan Governor Ueda, after raising rates by 25bps to 
1.25% in September, alluded to rising inflation pressures, 
stating the policy environment has shifted to a “new phase”. 
He added that the BoJ must now act to prevent inflation from 
overshooting, rather than simply trying to reach the 2% target. 
September’s BoJ decision came in a 7-2 split vote, with two 
members nominated by Prime Minister Takaichi calling for a 
hold. However, Ueda’s post -decision comments lead us to 
believe that the BoJ board will be comfortable with a 25bps 
rate hike every quarter until Q2 2027, taking the rate to 2%.
